or to Eq.(2.20)-(2.21) in
[
Chandrasekaran_Speranza2021
]
where it is assumed
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{d}\ell+d\boldsymbol{C}\,_{|\partial\Sigma}
– for some 1-form
\boldsymbol{C}
in
\Phi
, so a term
\iota_{X^{v}}\boldsymbol{C}
adds to the charge. In GR with
\Lambda=0
, (
211
) applies for
L^{\prime}=L_{\text{EGH}}+d\ell_{\text{YGH}}
, with
\ell_{\text{YGH}}
the York-Gibbons-Hawking boundary Lagrangian.

The relation (
210
) allows to investigate the possibility of defining a Poisson bracket of Noether charges.
For
X,Y\in\mathfrak{diff}(M)
generating
X^{v},Y^{v}\in\Gamma(V\phi)
, the standard prescription is:

\displaystyle\big\{Q_{\Sigma}(X;\phi),Q_{\Sigma}(Y;\phi)\big\}\vcentcolon=\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\Sigma}\big(X^{v},Y^{v}\big).

(212)

We need only to work out the right-hand side. For this, using (
208
) we have,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Theta}\big(X^{v},Y^{v}\big)=\iota_{Y^{v}}\iota_{X^{v}}\boldsymbol{\Theta}=-\iota_{Y^{v}}\left(\boldsymbol{d}\iota_{X^{v}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}+\boldsymbol{\alpha}(X)\right)

\displaystyle=-\boldsymbol{L}_{Y^{v}}\iota_{X^{v}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}+\iota_{Y^{v}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(X),

\displaystyle=\iota_{[X^{v},Y^{v}]}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}-\iota_{X^{v}}\boldsymbol{L}_{Y^{v}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}+\iota_{Y^{v}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(X),

\displaystyle=\iota_{([X,Y]_{\text{{\tiny$\mathfrak{diff}(M)$}}})^{v}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}-\iota_{X^{v}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(Y)+\iota_{Y^{v}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(X).

(213)

where we used (
39
) with (
2.2.2
) (or (
2.2.1
)/(
24
)), as well as (
) from appendix (
).
The last two terms can be related to the Lagrangian anomaly which, as stated below (
50
), satisfies an Abelian version of the 1-cocycle relation (consistency condition) (
48
):

\displaystyle\iota_{X^{v}}\boldsymbol{d}\alpha(Y;\phi)-\iota_{Y^{v}}\boldsymbol{d}\alpha(X;\phi)=\alpha\big([X,Y]_{\text{{\tiny$\mathfrak{diff}(M)$}}};\phi\big)

(214)

stemming from the commutation relation of Nijenhuis-Lie derivative (
40
)-(
2.2.2
) (or (
2.2.1
)/(
24
)). Indeed, using (
201
) we get:

\displaystyle\iota_{X^{v}}d\big(\boldsymbol{\alpha}(Y)+\iota_{Y}\boldsymbol{E}\big)-\iota_{Y^{v}}d\big(\boldsymbol{\alpha}(X)+\iota_{X}\boldsymbol{E}\big)=d\beta\big([X,Y]_{\text{{\tiny$\mathfrak{diff}(M)$}}};\phi\big),

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow

\displaystyle\quad\iota_{X^{v}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(Y)-\iota_{Y^{v}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(X)+\iota_{X^{v}}\iota_{Y}\boldsymbol{E}-\iota_{Y^{v}}\iota_{X}\boldsymbol{E}-\beta\big([X,Y]_{\text{{\tiny$\mathfrak{diff}(M)$}}};\phi\big)=\vcentcolon-d\mathscr{A}\big(\lfloor X,Y\!\rfloor;\phi\big).

(215)

The notation
d\mathscr{A}\big(\lfloor X,Y\!\rfloor;\phi\big)
is meant to indicate that it is bilinear and antisymmetric in
X,Y
(as the left-hand side shows).
Inserting this into (
213
), and using the definition (
203
) of the Noether current, we get

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Theta}\big(X^{v},Y^{v}\big)

\displaystyle=\iota_{([X,Y]_{\text{{\tiny$\mathfrak{diff}(M)$}}})^{v}}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}-\beta\big([X,Y]_{\text{{\tiny$\mathfrak{diff}(M)$}}};\phi\big)+d\mathscr{A}\big(\lfloor X,Y\!\rfloor;\phi\big)\ +\iota_{X^{v}}\iota_{Y}\boldsymbol{E}-\iota_{Y^{v}}\iota_{X}\boldsymbol{E},

\displaystyle=J\big([X,Y]_{\text{{\tiny$\mathfrak{diff}(M)$}}};\phi\big)+d\gamma\big([X,Y]_{\text{{\tiny$\mathfrak{diff}(M)$}}};\phi\big)+d\mathscr{A}\big(\lfloor X,Y\!\rfloor;\phi\big)\ +\iota_{X^{v}}\iota_{Y}\boldsymbol{E}-\iota_{Y^{v}}\iota_{X}\boldsymbol{E}.

(216)

Which gives the formal expression,

\displaystyle\big\{Q_{\Sigma}(X;\phi),Q_{\Sigma}(Y;\phi)\big\}=Q_{\Sigma}\big([X,Y]_{\text{{\tiny$\mathfrak{diff}(M)$}}};\phi\big)+\int_{\partial\Sigma}\mathscr{A}\big(\lfloor X,Y\!\rfloor;\phi\big)+\gamma\big([X,Y]_{\text{{\tiny$\mathfrak{diff}(M)$}}};\phi\big)+\int_{\Sigma}\iota_{X^{v}}\iota_{Y}\boldsymbol{E}-\iota_{Y^{v}}\iota_{X}\boldsymbol{E}.

(217)

Which is on-shell,

\displaystyle\big\{Q_{\Sigma}(X;\phi),Q_{\Sigma}(Y;\phi)\big\}=Q_{\Sigma}\big([X,Y]_{\text{{\tiny$\mathfrak{diff}(M)$}}};\phi\big)+\mathscr{C}\big(\lfloor X,Y\!\rfloor;\phi\big)_{\ |\mathcal{S}}\,,

(218)

defining the map

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}\big(\ \ ;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\phi}\big):\mathfrak{diff}(M)\times\mathfrak{diff}(M)

\displaystyle\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\Phi),

(219)

\displaystyle(X,Y)

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathscr{C}\big(\lfloor X,Y\!\rfloor;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\phi}\big)\vcentcolon=\int_{\partial\Sigma}\mathscr{A}\big(\lfloor X,Y\!\rfloor;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\phi}\big)+\gamma\big([X,Y]_{\text{{\tiny$\mathfrak{diff}(M)$}}};{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\phi}\big),

which is clearly bilinear and antisymmetric. If it can be shown that it furthermore satisfies

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}\big(\lfloor X,[Y,Z]_{\text{{\tiny$\mathfrak{diff}(M)$}}}\rfloor;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\phi}\big)+\mathscr{C}\big(\lfloor Y,[Z,X]_{\text{{\tiny$\mathfrak{diff}(M)$}}}\rfloor;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\phi}\big)+\mathscr{C}\big(\lfloor Z,[X,Y]_{\text{{\tiny$\mathfrak{diff}(M)$}}}\rfloor;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\phi}\big)\equiv 0,

(220)

then it would be a 2-cocycle on
\mathfrak{diff}(M)
, meaning that the Poisson bracket (
218
) is a Lie bracket
30
30

30
